--- original brief ---
# Codex verification brief — sentence-level claim check (commit 0c441e0)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. You may write scratch
files only under `audit_evidence_claims/`. End with a verdict line, exactly one of:

    VERDICT: APPROVE
    VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED

## What changed

`OncoKG.check_groundedness` (src/query.py) checks a *generated* answer against the
evidence retrieved for the question. Before this commit it only checked that names and
numbers appeared somewhere in the evidence, so "AstraZeneca has discontinued
osimertinib", "osimertinib does not act on EGFR" and "osimertinib is safe" passed.

Now, given `linker=self.link_entities`, it also calls `claims.check_claims`
(src/claims.py), which reads the answer sentence by sentence:

1. A stated relation (acts on / evaluates / sponsors) must hold between the same
   entities in an evidence statement of that relation.
2. Negations, comparisons, hedges and "develops/owns/markets" are reported unless the
   sentence is quoting an evidence statement about the same entities.
3. "phase N" and "N trials" must be stated for that sentence's own entities (a named
   trial is the most specific subject).
4. Every other content word must be grammar (FUNCTION list) or appear in an evidence
   statement about the sentence's entities.

Also: `link_entities` no longer joins several short words into a one-word drug
abbreviation ("is a" was read as "isa" = isatuximab).

The goal set by the owner: ordinary-word claims ("safe", "discontinued") and relations
must be caught. The design is deliberately strict: a true fact the graph does not hold
is reported as unverifiable, and a human reviewer decides.

## What to verify (reproduce everything; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **False negatives (most important).** Find answers containing a claim the retrieved
   evidence does not support that still return `grounded=True`. Use the real graph:
   `python src/app.py` exposes `Engine().check(question, answer)`; or call
   `OncoKG().check_groundedness(answer, evidence, entities, True, vocab, linker=kg.link_entities)`.
   Try at least: reversed relations, wrong drug–target / company–drug / trial–drug
   pairing in multi-entity sentences, pronouns ("it", "this drug"), relations stated
   with nouns ("an EGFR inhibitor", "AstraZeneca's osimertinib"), status/phase of the
   wrong trial, counts, negation spelled differently ("isn't", "no longer",
   "un-", "non-"), clinical claims made only of words that happen to be in evidence
   statements, claims split across sentences, lists, Korean or mixed text, markdown.
B. **False positives.** Faithful answers an LLM would plausibly write from the evidence
   (paraphrases, "is an inhibitor of", "is being tested in", lists) that are flagged.
   Strict is intended, but a check that flags most faithful answers is not useful —
   say how often it happens on realistic paraphrases of each demo question in
   `src/query.py:main`.
C. **Correctness of the code**: regexes, sentence splitting, the subject-inheritance
   rule for fragments of one line, `quoted()` bigram rule (can it be abused to launder a
   negation?), dedup in `check_groundedness` (one finding per value — can it hide a
   finding?), the `link_entities` change (does it break any multi-word drug name the
   pipeline should still link? check against the dictionary).
D. **Regressions**: `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (79 expected), and
   that the template answers for the demo questions, read as prose, still pass.
E. **Documentation honesty**: README.md / README.ko.md item 13, the docstrings in
   claims.py and query.py, web/template.html section 06, web/try.html box 3 — do they
   claim more than the code does?

## Report format

`BLOCKERS` (claims that pass and should not, or wrong code), `SHOULD FIX`, `NOTES`,
each with a reproducible input and the observed output. Then the verdict line.

---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

## BLOCKERS

Verified commit `0c441e015117fd9384fa54419ca484fb748dc70b` against the real local graph. **Unsupported claims still return `grounded=True`.** No tracked files were changed.

[Full report with exact inputs, outputs and evidence](<C:/Users/geoum/Dropbox/GY's Formal Workspace/2026 Job recruting/바이오리서치에이아이/oncokg/audit_evidence_claims/independent_verification_report.txt>) · [Reproduction script](<C:/Users/geoum/Dropbox/GY's Formal Workspace/2026 Job recruting/바이오리서치에이아이/oncokg/audit_evidence_claims/independent_reproduce.ps1>)

Question identifiers below denote these exact inputs:

| ID | Question |
|---|---|
| Q1 | `Which companies are developing osimertinib?` |
| Q2 | `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?` |
| Q3 | `What does osimertinib target?` |
| Q4 | `Which drugs act on ALK?` |
| Q5 | `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?` |
| Q6 | `What is the capital of France?` |

Unless stated otherwise, reproductions call `Engine().check(question, answer)`. **Every answer in this BLOCKERS section returned `grounded=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`.**

1. **Relations are checked without sentence direction or required endpoints.**  
   `src/claims.py:170`, `src/claims.py:183`, `src/claims.py:225` assign roles by entity type, permit empty pair loops, and exempt relation words from further checking.

   | Question | Exact answer |
   |---|---|
   | Q3 | `EGFR acts on osimertinib.` |
   | Q3 | `EGFR is an osimertinib inhibitor.` |
   | Q2 | `osimertinib evaluates NCT03909334.` |
   | Q5 | `osimertinib sponsors AstraZeneca.` |
   | Q3 | `Osimertinib sponsors EGFR.` |

   The retrieved edges support the opposite direction in the first four examples; none supports the fifth relation.

2. **Pronouns can lose their subject when the next sentence names an object.**  
   `src/claims.py:129` replaces inherited entities whenever *any* entity is found. Missing relation endpoints then pass unchecked.

   - Q3: `osimertinib acts on EGFR. It inhibits ALK.`
   - Q3: `osimertinib acts on EGFR. This drug inhibits ALK.`
   - Q2: `NCT03909334 evaluates osimertinib. It evaluates osimertinib and is phase 3.`

   The ALK claim passes both on one line and with a newline before `It`. The trial is **PHASE2**; mentioning osimertinib in the second sentence replaces the trial antecedent and admits other osimertinib trials’ phases.

   Controls: `osimertinib inhibits ALK.` fails, and `NCT03909334 evaluates osimertinib. It is phase 3.` fails.

3. **Evidence scope pools different entities, allowing phases, statuses and counts to migrate.**  
   `src/claims.py:138` selects statements mentioning **any** sentence entity; `src/claims.py:208` and `src/claims.py:218` accept a matching value anywhere in that pool.

   | Question | Exact answer | Retrieved evidence |
   |---|---|---|
   | Q2 | `NCT03909334 is phase 3 and NCT07680790 is phase 2.` | Phases are respectively **2** and **3**. |
   | Q2 | `NCT03909334 and NCT07680790 are recruiting.` | First is **ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING**; second is RECRUITING. |
   | Q5 | `AstraZeneca sponsors 10 NSCLC trials evaluating osimertinib.` | Osimertinib count is **14**; **10** belongs to carboplatin. |
   | Q4 | `PLB1003 acts on ALK with 13 NSCLC trials.` | PLB1003 count is **0**; **13** belongs to lorlatinib. |
   | Q4 | `PLB1003 is approved for all lung cancer and acts on ALK.` | PLB1003 has **PHASE_1**; APPROVAL comes from other ALK drugs. |

4. **`quoted()` launders development and negation claims through matching bigrams.**  
   `src/claims.py:148`, `src/claims.py:155`, `src/claims.py:201`.

   Q1 answers:

   - `AstraZeneca is developing the drug osimertinib.`
   - `AstraZeneca has evidence of developing osimertinib.`
   - `This is not evidence that AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib.`

   The evidence says **“a sponsorship, not evidence of developing the drug.”** Matching `developing the`, `of developing`, or `not evidence` exempts the changed claim without checking its meaning or negation scope.

5. **Hyphenated `non-` negation disappears.**  
   `non` is in `FUNCTION` at `src/claims.py:70`; tokenization at `src/claims.py:102` separates it from the following word.

   - Q3: `Osimertinib is a non-inhibitor of EGFR.`
   - Q2: `NCT07680790 is non-recruiting.`

   Evidence states EGFR inhibition and **RECRUITING**, respectively.

6. **Markdown brackets and non-English text can bypass claim checking entirely.**  
   `src/claims.py:87` removes arbitrary bracket contents; `src/claims.py:102` and `src/claims.py:111` only recognize ASCII words.

   All with Q3:

   - `Osimertinib [is safe and discontinued] acts on EGFR.`
   - `[osimertinib is safe](osimertinib) acts on EGFR.`
   - `오시머티닙은 안전하며 부작용이 없다.`
   - `osimertinib 은 EGFR 을 억제하지 않는다.`

   These are skipped claims, not merely difficult semantic paraphrases.

7. **Allowed words and relation synonyms still permit unsupported clinical claims.**

   - Q3: `Osimertinib is a treatment for all cancer.` passes because the clinical assertion consists of `FUNCTION` words (`src/claims.py:65`, `src/claims.py:225`). The evidence only states EGFR inhibition.
   - Question: `What does omaveloxolone target?`  
     Answer: `Omaveloxolone inhibits NFE2L2.`  
     Evidence: `OMAVELOXOLONE acts on NFE2L2 (activator)`.

   The second example exposes loss of action type: inhibition and other relation words map to generic `ACTS_ON` at `src/claims.py:35`; `src/claims.py:185` does not verify the stated action.

8. **Numeric regexes accept partial phases and miss ordinary count layouts.**

   | Question | Exact answer | Failure |
   |---|---|---|
   | Q4 | `PLB1003 is phase 13.` | Phase regex reads only **phase 1**; global number check finds 13 elsewhere. |
   | Q2 | `NCT07680790 is phase 3b.` | Regex validates **phase 3**; one-letter suffix is ignored. |
   | Q4 | `PLB1003 has 13 non small cell lung cancer trials.` | Count regex allows at most two intervening words. |
   | Q4 | `"- PLB1003: NSCLC trials: 13\n- lorlatinib: NSCLC trials: 0"` | Number-after-label layout bypasses count checking. |

   References: `src/claims.py:89`, `src/claims.py:206`, `src/claims.py:213`; global numeric handling at `src/query.py:496`. Actual PLB1003 and lorlatinib counts are **0** and **13**.

## SHOULD FIX

**Faithful paraphrases are rejected frequently.** Six hand-authored answers per demo question produced:

| Question | Faithful answers flagged |
|---|---:|
| Q1 | 2/6 |
| Q2 | 2/6 |
| Q3 | 1/6 |
| Q4 | 3/6 |
| Q5 | 2/6 |
| Q6: appropriate refusals | 6/6 |

That is **10/30 (33.3%)** for evidence-based answers, with refusals reported separately. This diagnostic corpus does **not** establish that most realistic answers fail, nor estimate a production LLM error rate.

Representative exact reproductions:

| Question | Answer | Observed output |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 | `AstraZeneca leads 14 trials investigating osimertinib.` | `grounded=False`; `wording: leads` |
| Q2 | `NCT03909334 is a phase 2 trial evaluating osimertinib and is active, not recruiting.` | False; `wording: active, recruiting` |
| Q2 | `NCT07680790 is a phase 3 trial evaluating Tagrisso.` | False; `drug_name: Tagrisso` |
| Q3 | `"Reported mechanism:\n- Osimertinib: inhibition of EGFR."` | False; `wording: inhibition` |
| Q4 | `CONTELTINIB acts on ALK with a maximum stage of phase 1/2.` | False; `number: 2`, `phase: phase 1/2` |
| Q4 | `"Agents acting on ALK:\n- LORLATINIB\n- CRIZOTINIB\n- ALECTINIB"` | False; `wording: alk` |
| Q6 | `This graph cannot answer that question.` | False; `negation: cannot`, `wording: answer, question` |

Causes include underscore-preserving status tokens (`src/claims.py:102`), missing lexical equivalents (`src/claims.py:227`), literal alias comparison (`src/query.py:529`), compound-phase normalization (`src/claims.py:208`), and colon-sensitive target linking (`src/query.py:120`, `src/query.py:171`). The Tagrisso rejection is inherited from the existing overlap check.

**Correct paired clauses are rejected by the Cartesian-product relation check.**  
Using the union of unmodified evidence retrieved for `What does osimertinib target?` and `What does crizotinib target?`:

- Answer: `osimertinib acts on EGFR and crizotinib acts on ALK.`
- Output: `grounded=False`, with invented obligations `CRIZOTINIB — acts on — EGFR` and `OSIMERTINIB — acts on — ALK`.
- Splitting the same clauses into two sentences returns `grounded=True`.

The same behavior was reproduced for correct trial–drug and company–drug pairs. Cause: `src/claims.py:183`.

**Dedup hides subsequent claim contexts.**  
Q3, answer `Osimertinib is safe. EGFR is safe.` produces two raw `safe` findings, but `src/query.py:545` retains only the first sentence’s finding. Final output remains `grounded=False`; dedup alone cannot turn a nonempty finding list into a pass.

**Documentation promises exceed the implementation.**

- `README.md:508`, `README.ko.md:354`: categorical relation/content-word guarantees omit the demonstrated bypasses.
- `src/claims.py:27`: “negation words are always reported” directly contradicts the quotation exception and observed results.
- `src/query.py:444`, `src/query.py:458`: verification guarantees are stronger than the actual checks.
- `web/template.html:263`, `web/try.html:69`: claims about catching unsupported relations and clinical statements need qualification.
- `web/try.html:140`: the success message says every sentence’s relations and wording match the evidence—even for the passing counterexamples above.

The existing “words, not meaning” caveat does not adequately describe discarded text, unchecked endpoints, or transferred values.

## NOTES

- **Regression suite:** `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` → **79 passed in 5.96s**.
- **Templates:** all **5/5 evidence template bodies** pass when explicitly checked as generated prose, after removing `Scope:` exactly as `src/query.py:389` does. Full templates including Scope fail on metadata counts and `only`. The France refusal fails generated-prose checking; normal `ask()` accepts it through a separate refusal path.
- **Linker change:** compared **2,634 unique stored multiword aliases**, from **7,610 dictionary keys / 1,066 drugs**, against the previous behavior. **No intended links were lost.** `is a` changes from ISATUXIMAB to no match. `AZD 9291`, `osimertinib mesylate`, `trastuzumab deruxtecan`, and `pemetrexed disodium` retain their links.
- **Successful controls:** ordinary `safe`, `discontinued`, `not`, `isn't`, `isn’t`, `no longer`, and tested `un-` forms are flagged. `Osimertinib is an inhibitor of EGFR.` and `Osimertinib is being tested in NCT03909334.` pass. Explicit wrong named drug–target, trial–drug and company–drug pairs are rejected. `AstraZeneca's osimertinib.` is flagged as wording.
- **Execution-scope exception:** the first pytest run inherited system TEMP/TMP; test `TemporaryDirectory()` contexts created and automatically removed files outside the permitted scratch directory. I reran the exact command with TEMP/TMP confined to `audit_evidence_claims/`; all 79 tests passed. Bytecode and pytest cache writes were disabled. Final tracked-file diff is empty.

# Independent claim-check audit, round 2

Reviewed HEAD **a12762b**, against the real local graph, with diff `git diff 0c441e0...HEAD`. No tracked files were edited. All new scratch files are under `audit_evidence_claims/r2/`; the pre-existing `brief.md` and `log.txt` were left alone.

## BLOCKERS

**Unsupported claims still return `grounded=True`, `applicable=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`.** Every attack below was executed; these are not hypothetical parser concerns. Except the explicitly identified union-evidence cases, they use `Engine().check(question, answer)` or the equivalent direct `check_groundedness` call with that question's unmodified retrieved evidence. Truth here means support in the retrieved evidence, not an external medical judgment.

Question keys are exact inputs:

| Key | Question |
|---|---|
| Q1 | `Which companies are developing osimertinib?` |
| Q2 | `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?` |
| Q3 | `What does osimertinib target?` |
| Q4 | `Which drugs act on ALK?` |
| Q5 | `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?` |
| Q6 | `What is the capital of France?` |
| QC | `What does crizotinib target?` |
| QF | `What does fulvestrant target?` |

### B1. The original reversed noun relation is NOT FIXED; passive detection also reverses active claims

| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| Q3 | `EGFR is an osimertinib inhibitor.` | True; `[]` |
| Q3 | `EGFR is what inhibited osimertinib.` | True; `[]` |
| Q2 | `Osimertinib is what evaluated NCT03909334.` | True; `[]` |
| Q5 | `Osimertinib is what sponsored AstraZeneca.` | True; `[]` |

The sole Q3 evidence is `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`, the opposite direction. Q2 says the trial evaluates the drug; Q5 says the company sponsors trials of the drug.

The noun fallback at `src/claims.py:407-411` fills an absent endpoint using entity type after `_group` selects the nearest entity (`:382-383`), reconstructing the supported inverse instead of checking the written subject. The passive heuristic at `:401-404` treats any `BE` token within three words as an auxiliary. In `is what inhibited`, `inhibited` is an active past-tense verb in a cleft, but the checker reverses it.

The first answer is **an exact R1 reproduction**, not a new variant. It is absent from the regression attack list at `tests/test_claims.py:81-122`, despite the repair table's statement that all five R1 direction examples were repaired. Sources: `replay_main_results.json` (`reverse_noun`), `spec_probe_results.json` (`passive_cleft`, `passive_cleft_trial`, `passive_cleft_sponsor`).

### B2. A repeated preposition or an adverb hides an additional relation endpoint

All with Q3:

| Exact answer | Observed |
|---|---|
| `Osimertinib acts on EGFR and on ALK.` | True; `[]` |
| `Osimertinib is an inhibitor of EGFR and of ALK.` | True; `[]` |
| `Osimertinib inhibits EGFR and also ALK.` | True; `[]` |
| Control: `Osimertinib acts on EGFR and ALK.` | False; `relation: OSIMERTINIB — acts — ALK` |

No Q3 evidence mentions ALK. `_group` stops at `on`, `of`, or `also` (`src/claims.py:342-357`). `_clauses` does not split an elided predicate fragment (`:324-341`), and the later wording pass exempts all these grammar/relation words (`:552-553`). The extra named entity is left with no relation obligation. This also affects `target of`: with Q3+QC evidence, `EGFR is a target of osimertinib and of crizotinib.` passes although there is no retrieved crizotinib–EGFR edge. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `endpoint_preposition`, `endpoint_noun`, `endpoint_adverb`, `endpoint_control`, `noun_target_second`.

### B3. Pronouns resolve before the preceding clause is processed; verbatim evidence loses trial subjects

**Clause timing.** Use the unmodified union of evidence retrieved for Q3 and QC, with their linked question entities and the normal vocabulary/linker:

```text
Osimertinib acts on EGFR. Crizotinib acts on ALK and it inhibits EGFR.
```

Observed: True; `[]`. The union contains osimertinib–EGFR and crizotinib–ALK, but no crizotinib–EGFR edge. Replacing `and` with `.`, so that the last sentence is `It inhibits EGFR.`, gives False with `relation: CRIZOTINIB — inhibits — EGFR`. Omitting `it` (`... and inhibits EGFR.`) also correctly fails. The typed variant `... while this drug inhibits EGFR.` wrongly passes.

`_tokenise` resolves the entire unit at `src/claims.py:228`, before `_clauses` and the antecedent updates at `:259-261`; resolution at `:299-300` therefore uses the previous unit's osimertinib. The clause reader updates state too late to change the already substituted pronoun. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `pronoun_stale`, `pronoun_stale2`, `pronoun_fresh`, `elided`.

**Verbatim antecedent.** With Q2:

```text
NCT03909334 (PHASE2, ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB. It is phase 3.
```

Observed: True; `[]`. The named trial is PHASE2. In contrast, `NCT03909334 evaluates osimertinib. It is phase 3.` correctly fails. At `src/claims.py:221-226`, the exact-statement shortcut chooses the first result of `linker(bare)` as subject. The linker does not produce trial entities; NCT handling exists separately at `:274-275`. Thus the copied statement installs OSIMERTINIB as antecedent, admitting phase 3 from other trials of that drug. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `verbatim_antecedent_trial`, `paraphrase_trial_wrong_phase_control`.

### B4. Numeric ownership still migrates between drugs and between attribute types

| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| Q5 | `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 10 trials with carboplatin.` | True; `[]` |
| Q5 | Control: `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 10 trials.` | False; `number: 10` |
| Q5 | Diagnostic: `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 14 trials with carboplatin.` | False; `number: 14` |
| Q4 | `PLB1003 has 1 clinical trial.` | True; `[]` |

The retrieved AstraZeneca count is **14 for osimertinib**, **10 for carboplatin**; it does not establish ten trials combining them. `_owner` searches after a number before looking before it (`src/claims.py:460-468`, `:524`). The appended carboplatin captures the count even though the claim is about osimertinib; the incorrect 10 passes and the osimertinib count 14 fails. The 14 example is a diagnostic of assignment, not a claim that the combination is supported.

PLB1003 evidence explicitly says `max stage PHASE_1; 0 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph`. Numeric checking matches any number in a scoped statement (`:524-525`), so phase **1** satisfies a claimed count of **1** despite the count being **0**. This is a specific, reproducible limitation of counting, not merely a distant-entity issue. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `number_drug_other`, `count_after`, `count_vs_14`, `count_morph`.

### B5. Action validation matches `agonist` inside `antagonist`

Question QF; sole evidence: `FULVESTRANT acts on ESR1 (antagonist)`.

- `Fulvestrant activates ESR1.` → True; `[]`.
- `Fulvestrant is an agonist of ESR1.` → True; `[]`.
- Control: `Fulvestrant is an antagonist of ESR1.` → True, correctly.

`ACTION_WORDS` includes `agonist` (`src/claims.py:73-74`); `:454-455` uses substring containment rather than matching the action token/field. Consequently the action described in the evidence satisfies its opposite. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `action_antagonist`, `action_noun_agonist`, `action_control_antagonist`.

### B6. Heading handling discards claims; the bare-item verbatim pass bypasses joined headings

All with Q3 and its sole EGFR-inhibition statement:

| Exact answer (`\n` denotes a newline) | Observed |
|---|---|
| `osimertinib is safe:` | True; `[]` |
| `osimertinib is discontinued:\nOsimertinib acts on EGFR.` | True; `[]` |
| `these drugs are safe:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | True; `[]` |
| `these drugs do not act on EGFR:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | True; `[]` |
| `EGFR acts on these drugs:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | True; `[]` |
| Control: `these drugs are safe:\n- osimertinib` | False; `wording: safe` |

`_units` defers any non-bullet line ending in a colon and never emits it if no bullet follows (`src/claims.py:170-188`). Thus ordinary content words can disappear entirely. When a bullet does follow, joining does retain the heading (`:177-183`), but the shortcut tests **either** the combined unit **or the original bare item** against evidence (`:221`). A verbatim evidence item exempts an unsafe, negated, or reversed heading. These are separate paths; fixing dangling headings alone would leave the bare-item bypass. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `discarded_heading`, `discarded_heading_before_prose`, `verbatim_under_unsafe_heading`, `verbatim_under_negated_heading`, `verbatim_under_reversed_heading`, `ordinary_bullet_heading_control`.

### B7. The META exception accepts biomedical claims, including an invented phase with zero evidence

| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| Q6 | `This trial is phase 4.` | True; `[]`; **evidence_count=0** |
| Q2 | `This trial is phase 3.` | True; `[]` |
| Q2 | `This drug is not in trials.` | True; `[]` |
| Q1 | `It has 14 trials.` | True; `[]` |

These are not graph refusals. An unresolved pronoun should be reported, as the docstring explicitly says (`src/claims.py:22-24`). The early pass at `:233-236` permits every `FUNCTION`/negation word and examines only word tokens, ignoring numbers. `phase` and `trials` are grammar words. The global phase check also skips any one-digit phase whenever a linker is present (`src/query.py:504-508`), even if evidence is empty. A missing-subject phase fragment takes a similar early exit (`claims.py:247-254`).

Control: Q3 / `It inhibits EGFR.` correctly produces `no_antecedent: it`; the bug is the overbroad refusal exception, not an intentional general policy to accept unresolved claims. A legitimate `This graph cannot answer that question.` passes. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `meta_france_unsupported_phase`, `meta_missing_typed_phase`, `meta_missing_negation`, `meta_missing_count`, `no_antecedent_control`.

### B8. Refused product spans become opaque entities, swallowing qualifiers and negation

All with Q3; all return True; `[]`:

- `osimertinib is liposomal.`
- `osimertinib is a biosimilar.`
- `Osimertinib is not liposomal.`
- `liposomal Tagrisso.`

The retrieved statement establishes only the EGFR action, not these product claims. `src/query.py:128-141` deliberately emits `Refused` spans and includes neighboring words. `src/claims.py:278-288` accepts them as entity placeholders; `:231-240` rejects `Missing` but does not reject `Refused`. The consumed span, including `not`, is no longer checked as wording or negation. In the Tagrisso example, the new alias exception (`query.py:495-499`, `:543-544`) admits the isolated brand token even though its enclosing product was refused. Sources: `standards_probe.json`, cases `refused_liposomal`, `refused_biosimilar`, `refused_negation`, `refused_alias`.

### B9. Citation stripping still accepts arbitrary content after `ORG:`

Q3 / `Osimertinib [ORG:is safe] acts on EGFR.` → True; `[]`.

Q3 / `Osimertinib [ORG:inhibits ALK and] acts on EGFR.` → True; `[]`.

The regex `ORG:[^\]]+` (`src/claims.py:136-137`) treats arbitrary bracket text as a citation, and `_units` deletes it at `:167`. It neither validates a source identifier nor checks membership in retrieved records. The ordinary R1 bracket example is now caught, but adding the citation prefix restores a text-deletion bypass. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `citation_embedded_claim`, `citation_embedded_wrong_relation`.

Evidence-answer false positives improved from **10/30 (33.3%) to 1/30 (3.3%)**. This small hand-authored diagnostic corpus does not estimate a production LLM rate or support saying that most faithful answers fail. The remaining evidence-answer rejection is Q5 / `AstraZeneca supports trials studying osimertinib in NSCLC.` → False, `wording: supports`; the repair notes explicitly identify this as intentional strictness.

The first is the same within-unit antecedent timing error as B3. This replays the original gate-on/gate-off experiment, not an old-commit execution.

The question `Which drugs act on ALK!` (likewise `:` or `;`) still links no entity and yields `intent=unsupported`, while `?` works (`query.py:120`). This is an unaddressed question-path limitation, not a new linker regression.

# Round 3 independent claim-check review

## BLOCKERS

Audited HEAD `8d4a6060c6d48e6d1d20a2d1470238539bb8fcf9`, with repairs in `a12762b` and `9a80fcb`, against the real local graph. **All exact previously reported blocker inputs are now rejected, but new unsupported claims still return `grounded=True`.** No tracked file was edited. Scratch artifacts are confined to `audit_evidence_claims/r3/`; pre-existing `brief.md` and `log.txt` were not edited.

Question shorthand below always denotes these exact strings:

| ID | Question |
|---|---|
| Q1 | `Which companies are developing osimertinib?` |
| Q2 | `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?` |
| Q3 | `What does osimertinib target?` |
| Q4 | `Which drugs act on ALK?` |
| Q5 | `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?` |
| Q6 | `What is the capital of France?` |

All new blockers use `Engine().check(question, answer)`, without fabricated evidence or a union of questions. `True; []` means `checks.grounded=True`, `checks.unsupported_claims=[]`. Evidence support means support in the **retrieved evidence for that question**, not whether a claim might be true elsewhere. The README's explicitly listed single-statement rearrangement and unknown-name residues are considered separately below.

### B1. Plural pronouns shrink a coordinated subject to one drug

Q4:

| Exact answer | Observed |
|---|---|
| `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 act on ALK and they are approved.` | **True; []** |
| `PLB1003 and LORLATINIB act on ALK and they are approved.` | False; `wording: approved` |
| `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 act on ALK and PLB1003 is approved.` | False; `wording: approved` |

Retrieved evidence gives LORLATINIB `max stage APPROVAL`, but PLB1003 `max stage PHASE_1`. Both have supported ALK edges. The first answer extends approval to **both** drugs; reversing their order preserves that claim but changes the result.

`_State.note` stores only `real[0]` as subject (`src/claims.py:243-248`). `they` is an untyped pronoun (`:134`) and resolves to that single subject (`:298-302`). The second clause is therefore checked only against LORLATINIB's approval. The same error occurs across sentences when the second sentence starts with lowercase `they`; capitalized `They` happens to be rejected by the global capitalized-name check, not by correct plural resolution.

This is outside README 13's listed residue: approval is transferred across **two drugs and separate evidence statements**, rather than rearranging one statement about the same entities. Reproductions: [root3_probe.json](root3_probe.json), IDs `pronoun_same_clause`, `pronoun_same_clause_swap`, `pronoun_explicit_control`, `pronoun_two_sentences`.

### B2. Covering every entity in a relation does not prevent cross-drug count borrowing

Q5:

| Exact answer | Observed |
|---|---|
| `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 10 trials evaluating carboplatin.` | **True; []** |
| `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 14 trials evaluating carboplatin.` | False; `number: 14` |
| `AstraZeneca sponsors 10 trials evaluating carboplatin and osimertinib.` | **True; []** |
| `AstraZeneca sponsors 10 trials evaluating osimertinib and carboplatin.` | False; `number: 10` |
| `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib and carboplatin in 10 trials.` | **True; []** |

The evidence separately states **14** AstraZeneca trials evaluating OSIMERTINIB and **10** evaluating CARBOPLATIN. It establishes neither ten osimertinib trials nor ten trials evaluating both drugs. The reordered coordinated answers have the same ten-trial assertion but different results. The 14-trial combination is a diagnostic control, not a claim that the combination is supported.

`_owner` picks one nearest entity (`src/claims.py:594-604`); numeric `scoped` requires only that entity plus the first clause entity (`:610-618`), and the count check uses that scope (`:672-684`). Both drugs can independently satisfy relation checks (`:540-565`, `:580-586`), so `unrelated_entity` (`:345-350`) has nothing to reject. The R2 example with `with carboplatin` is fixed only because carboplatin is uncovered; adding a checked evaluation predicate restores the original numerical problem.

This mixes distinct evidence statements and is outside the explicit single-statement residue. Reproductions: [numeric3_probe.json](numeric3_probe.json), IDs `count_swap_tail`, `count_swap_tail_control`, `count_coordinated`, `count_coordinated_reverse`; [root3_probe.json](root3_probe.json), `count_shared_group`.

### B3. Verbatim list items still weaken heading validation

Q3, sole evidence: `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`.

| Exact answer (`\n` is a newline) | Observed |
|---|---|
| `these drugs are sponsors of trials:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | **True; []** |
| `these drugs are activators:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | **True; []** |
| `these drugs inhibit EGFR with ALK:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | **True; []** |
| `Osimertinib is a sponsor of trials.` | False; `relation_incomplete: sponsor` |
| `Osimertinib is an activator.` | False; `relation_incomplete: activator` |
| `Osimertinib inhibits EGFR with ALK.` | False; `unrelated_entity: ALK` |

The first heading attributes trial sponsorship to the drug; the second calls an inhibitor an activator. Neither assertion is supported by Q3 evidence. The `with ALK` sentence is a secondary attachment diagnostic; the clear sponsorship assertion alone is sufficient.

The copied item fills the heading's subject, then calls `_check_unit(..., lenient=True)` (`src/claims.py:271-287`). Lenient mode disables missing-endpoint findings (`:567-572`), the fragment rule (`:338`), and uncovered-entity findings (`:345`). Without endpoints, action-type validation at `:587-590` is never reached. Thus headings are visited, but do not satisfy the same constraints as ordinary prose. The exact R2 unsafe/negated/reversed headings are fixed; these are new cases.

The new heading contributes `sponsors`, `trials`, or `activators`, absent from the sole evidence statement. This is neither the intentional copied-line exception nor the one-statement rearrangement residue. Reproductions: [root3_probe.json](root3_probe.json), `heading_sponsors_full`, `heading_activators_full`, `heading_with_alk` and corresponding `plain_*` controls.

### B4. Grammar and disease-context exemptions allow ordinary clinical claims without evidence

| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| Q3 | `Osimertinib is in clinical trials.` | **True; []** |
| Q3 | `Osimertinib is in NSCLC trials.` | **True; []** |
| Q3 | `Osimertinib is an NSCLC drug.` | **True; []** |
| Q3 | `EGFR is a drug.` | **True; []** |
| Q6 | `There are clinical trials.` | **True; []; evidence_count=0** |

Q3's sole evidence contains only osimertinib's EGFR inhibition; it contains no clinical-trial or disease assertion. Q6 retrieves nothing. These are substantive claims, not graph refusals.

`clinical`, `trial(s)`, `drug(s)` and entity-type nouns are in `FUNCTION` (`src/claims.py:104-116`). With an entity they bypass wording validation (`:721-722`); without an entity, the no-subject branch reports nothing when all words are grammar (`:329-334`). The `nsclc` rule permits adjacency to `trials`/`drug(s)` or a preceding `in`, then removes the token without requiring disease evidence (`:690-699`). `Osimertinib is for lung cancer.` correctly fails, but changing the construction to `is an NSCLC drug` passes.

Related unchecked copulas show the fragment rule's limit: Q4 / `PLB1003 is LORLATINIB.` and Q2 / `NCT03909334 is NCT07680790.` both pass. `is` counts as a predicate, so the fragment guard (`:338-344`) does not apply, while it is not a checked relation. No retrieved statement equates these separate entities.

The core clinical examples add words absent from Q3 evidence, and Q6 has no statement to rearrange. They fall outside README 13's two explicit known-pass residues. Reproductions: [root3_probe.json](root3_probe.json), `clinical_trial_unsupported`, `nsclc_trial_unsupported`, `indication_drug`, `zero_evidence_clinical`, `fragment_drug_identity`, `fragment_trial_identity`; [docs3_focused_results.json](docs3_focused_results.json), `focused_07` for `EGFR is a drug.`.

## SHOULD FIX

### S1. Faithful evidence answers mostly pass, with natural forms still rejected

The original R1 corpus was replayed unchanged. A separate fresh corpus contains six hand-authored faithful answers per demo question. These are diagnostic samples, not a production LLM error-rate estimate; they should not be pooled as independent statistical samples.

|---|---:|---:|
| Q1 | 6/6 | 5/6 |
| Q2 | 6/6 | 4/6 |
| Q3 | 6/6 | 4/6 |
| Q4 | 6/6 | 6/6 |
| Q5 | 5/6 | 5/6 |
| Evidence answers | **29/30 (96.7%)** | **24/30 (80%)** |
| Q6 refusals, separate | 4/6 | 2/6 |

Thus the tested evidence answers satisfy the **mostly-pass** part of the bar. The verdict is driven by B1–B4 and the incomplete documented residue, not by a claim that most faithful answers fail.

Representative exact failures:

| Q | Exact faithful answer | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| Q3 | `Osimertinib targets EGFR and inhibits it.` | False; `relation_direction: OSIMERTINIB inhibits OSIMERTINIB` |
| Q2 | `NCT03909334 evaluates osimertinib in phase 2.` | False; `phase: phase 2` |
| Q2 | `NCT03909334 is a phase II trial evaluating osimertinib.` | False; `wording: ii` |
| Q5 | `AstraZeneca sponsors 14 NSCLC trials evaluating osimertinib and 10 NSCLC trials evaluating carboplatin.` | False; `number: 10` |

| Q3 | `Osimertinib (Tagrisso, AZD 9291, AZD9291) acts on EGFR.` | False; clause `number: 9291`, `wording: azd` |

Object pronouns are assigned the prior subject (`claims.py:298-302`). A phase after the drug selects drug max-stage evidence even when the sentence's named trial has the stated phase (`:657-664`). Roman phases are parsed (`:647-648`) but remain in the later wording list (`:701-726`). Repeated evaluation clauses split away the company owner (`:420-439`, `:614-617`). The global numeric-name exemption calls the deduplicating linker once (`query.py:508-513`), while claim tokenization permits only three alias-linking rounds (`claims.py:375`), producing separate repeated-alias failures.

`AZD-9291 acts on EGFR.` and `Osimertinib (Tagrisso, AZD 9291) acts on EGFR.` pass. The correct count-elision form `AstraZeneca sponsors 14 trials of osimertinib and 10 trials of carboplatin.` passes. ### S2. Documentation is substantially more candid, but still does not cover known passes

`README.md:508-524` and `README.ko.md:354-362` now explicitly describe a word-order pattern checker with unseen-shape limitations. `src/query.py:448-466` no longer promises semantic verification. `web/template.html:263-265` and `web/try.html:69-72` include the caveat; `web/try.html:142-145` says nothing was flagged and expressly disclaims proof of correctness. Those are improvements, and the old blanket documentation criticism should not be repeated unchanged.

Nevertheless, the **explicit known-pass list** in `README.md:517-519` covers rearranging one evidence statement and an unknown non-INN drug name. B1–B4 involve multiple statements, skipped heading obligations, new clinical vocabulary, or no evidence. A generic warning that unseen shapes can fail does not specifically list these residues under the requested approval bar.

The `claims.py:10-27` docstring frames its rules as what a clause **must satisfy to pass**: all relation entities covered, both endpoints identified, values scoped, and content supported. B1–B4 show additional exceptions beyond the two named at `:29-33`. Revise the universal phrasing and explicit residue or repair these behaviors before approval. The R2 repair table correctly retracts the old reversed-noun repair claim (`CODEX_CLAIMS_R2_VERDICT.md:6-7`); the exact prior direction cases now fail.

No separate repository coding-standard document was found, and no standalone style violation is asserted. This is a behavioral/specification and documentation review, not a source-code change; the instruction to compare changed source against KOA reference folders is therefore not triggered. Separate documentation/standards reasoning is in [docs3_review.md](docs3_review.md).

| Trial phase versus drug max stage | Wrong named-trial phases rejected; no new out-of-residue phase acceptance demonstrated. Natural post-object trial phase and Roman phases fail (S1). |
| Count rule | Exact old count attacks rejected; coordinated/cross-relation ownership still fails (B2). |
| Elided-repeat group extension | Correct `of 14 trials of osimertinib and 10 trials of carboplatin` passes, as does the three-drug 14/10/8 form; swapped values fail. Repeated `evaluating` form falsely fails (S1). |
| Per-clause/question-seeded pronouns | Old stale antecedents fixed. Q3 `It inhibits EGFR.` passes; `It inhibits ALK.` fails; `This trial is phase 3.` has no antecedent. Plural and object pronouns remain defective (B1/S1). |
| Headings under verbatim items | Old unsafe/negated/reversed headings fail; lenient relation omissions remain (B3). |
| `nsclc` indication rule | `is for lung cancer` fails; `is an NSCLC drug` and unsupported NSCLC-trial claims pass (B4). |
| Iterative aliases / name digits | Original aliases and spaced digits fixed; repeated numeric aliases/4th spelling still fail (S1). Standalone invented `9291 trials` remains rejected. |
| README 13 / docstrings / UI | Caveats improved; explicit residue and pass invariants remain incomplete (S2). |

### Already documented or weaker residues, not independent approval blockers

- Q4 / `PLB1003 has NSCLC trials: 1.` -> True, []; count detection looks only ahead from the number (`claims.py:674-682`), so PHASE_1 supplies 1 despite zero trials. Q4 / `LORLATINIB is approved in NSCLC.` also passes. Both plausibly fit the acknowledged one-statement rearrangement residue and are **not** used independently to reject approval.
- Q3 / `Osimertinib [ORG:is-safe] acts on EGFR.` and `[ORG:does-not-inhibit]` variants pass. `RE_CITE` accepts identifier-shaped content (`claims.py:153-155`) and removes it (`:197`) without retrieved-ID membership validation. These are weaker claim examples because they look like citations; the whitespace-containing R2 cases are fixed. Exact variants are retained in [docs3_focused_results.json](docs3_focused_results.json).
- `_flat` treats punctuation/case variants of evidence as copied lines, rather than byte-for-byte verbatim text. Tested punctuation variants did not establish an additional out-of-residue clinical claim.

### Regression, template, dictionary and filesystem verification

- `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **175 passed in 5.97s**, exit 0, empty stderr. The old brief's 79 and R2's 128 are previous suite sizes. UTF-8 mode and bytecode suppression were set; TEMP/TMP stayed under `r3/replay3_temp`.

Full Scope-inclusive templates and the France refusal template still fail generated checking as before; normal `ask()` has its separate refusal path. Exact template text/results: [replay3_faithful_results.json](replay3_faithful_results.json).
- Dictionary: **7,610 keys, 1,066 drugs, 2,634 unique multiword aliases**. The original abbreviation gate loses **zero** intended links; `is a` stays unlinked. The separate linker-to-tokenizer sweep has **2,566** aliases linking an intended ID and **five unchanged tokenizer discrepancies** (two parenthesized chemical names, three overlapping parent/salt names). These are not five new R3 linker regressions. See [numeric3_dictionary.json](numeric3_dictionary.json).
- Question punctuation limitation persists: `Which drugs act on ALK!` (also `:` / `;`) links no target; `?` works. Answer punctuation is handled separately. No new regression is claimed.
- Tracked and staged diffs are empty. Source hashes and final artifact integrity checks are recorded in [r3_verification.json](r3_verification.json). All review-created files are under `r3/`; no source repairs, commits, network calls, or model generation were performed.

# Round 4 independent claim-check review

## BLOCKERS

Reviewed HEAD `2de10d95295e94c24060648ee01a195496df7acb` against parent `8d4a6060c6d48e6d1d20a2d1470238539bb8fcf9`, using the real local graph. **Every exact previously reported blocker is now rejected, but new unsupported claims still pass outside README item 13's explicit residues.** Regression tests pass: **213/213**. No tracked files were edited; all review-created files are under `audit_evidence_claims/r4/`.

Question keys below are exact inputs:

| Key | Question |
|---|---|
| Q1 | `Which companies are developing osimertinib?` |
| Q2 | `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?` |
| Q3 | `What does osimertinib target?` |
| Q4 | `Which drugs act on ALK?` |
| Q5 | `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?` |
| Q6 | `What is the capital of France?` |

All blocker answers below were run through `Engine().check(question, answer)` with unmodified retrieved evidence. **Each returns `grounded=True`, `applicable=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`.** The standard is support in this retrieval, not external clinical truth. Exact full outputs and evidence are preserved in [root4_confirmation.json](root4_confirmation.json), [root4_probe.json](root4_probe.json), and the linked specialist artifacts. Twenty-five selected outcomes were independently rerun by the root reviewer after the parallel reviews.

### B1. Heading substitution deletes the unsupported clinical assertion

Q3, exact answers (`\n` denotes a newline):

| Answer | Observed |
|---|---|
| `The following safe drugs:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor).` | True; [] |
| `The following discontinued drugs:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor).` | True; [] |
| `The following safe drugs:\n- Osimertinib` | True; [] |
| Control: `These drugs are safe:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor).` | False; `wording: safe` |

The only evidence is `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`. Neither safety nor discontinuation is supported. At `src/claims.py:253-255`, `_join` substitutes `these|the following|following` **plus any optional alphabetic word**. It removes `safe` or `discontinued` before the checker sees the heading. Both ordinary units (`:296`) and copied-line headings (`:319-321`) use this function. Removing lenient mode cannot recover an erased word.

The same defect drops `terminated` from Q2 / `The following terminated trials:\n- NCT07680790 (PHASE3, RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB.` The retrieved status is RECRUITING. Wrong-entity joined headings are otherwise rejected, isolating this to substitution rather than the dash exemption. These assertions introduce content absent from the evidence, so the one-statement rearrangement residue does not cover them.

Artifacts: `root4_confirmation.json` IDs `heading_safe`, `heading_discontinued`, `heading_safe_bare`, `heading_safe_control`; [docs_findings.md](docs_findings.md), D-B1 and its exact controls.

### B2. Plural handling still loses members after expansion, in objects, and in elided subjects

| Q | Exact answer | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| Q4 | `PLB1003 and LORLATINIB act on ALK and they act on ALK and they are approved.` | True; [] |
| Q4 | `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 act on ALK and are approved.` | True; [] |
| Q5 | `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib and carboplatin. It sponsors them in 10 trials.` | True; [] |
| Q4 control | `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 act on ALK and they are approved.` | False; `wording: approved` |

PLB1003 has `max stage PHASE_1`, whereas LORLATINIB has `max stage APPROVAL`. Q5 separately gives AstraZeneca 14 osimertinib trials and 10 carboplatin trials; it does not support the ten-trial assertion about both drugs.

Three related causes:

- Each plural member is checked at `src/claims.py:352-356`, but each check updates shared state at `:425-426`. `_State.note` (`:271-277`) replaces the group with the current singleton. A later `they` sees only the last drug. Reversing the initial drugs in the three-clause example rejects approval. The lowercase multi-sentence equivalent also passes.
- Object-position `them` receives `want='Drug'` at `:340-344`, bypassing the plural branch's `want is None` condition (`:345`). `:349-350` then uses the last drug stored in `by_type`, CARBOPLATIN. This loses OSIMERTINIB before both relation and count checking.
- An elided subject uses only `st.subject` (`:388-391`), so `and are approved` checks the first drug, not the coordinated group.

These claims transfer attributes across different drugs and evidence statements; they are not the listed one-statement residue. Artifacts: `root4_confirmation.json` IDs `plural_chain`, `plural_implicit`, `object_count`, `plural_original_fixed`; [spec_findings.md](spec_findings.md), B1/B2.

### B3. `trials: N` bypasses the new count ambiguity check

Q5:

| Exact answer | Observed |
|---|---|
| `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib and carboplatin in trials: 10.` | True; [] |
| Control: `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib and carboplatin in 10 trials.` | False; `count_ambiguous: OSIMERTINIB / CARBOPLATIN` |

The evidence gives separate 14/10 counts, with no ten-trial count for both drugs. The ordinary number checker recognizes a preceding trial label at `src/claims.py:798-802`, but `count_ambiguous` only looks for a trial word **after** the number (`:819-820`). The count is therefore validated against the final drug alone. Correct separate `14 trials of osimertinib and 10 trials of carboplatin` passes.

Artifacts: `root4_confirmation.json` IDs `colon_count`, `prefix_count_control`; [spec_probe2.json](spec_probe2.json), `count_label_shared_same`; [spec_controls.json](spec_controls.json). This is another cross-statement count transfer, outside the explicit residue.

### B4. A shared phase or status validates only one trial

Q2, each answer returns True; []:

```text
NCT03909334 and NCT07680790 are recruiting trials of osimertinib.
```

```text
NCT03909334 and NCT07680790 are phase 3 trials of osimertinib.
```

```text
Recruiting trials evaluating osimertinib include NCT07680790 and NCT03909334.
```

The evidence is explicit:

```text
NCT03909334 (PHASE2, ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB
NCT07680790 (PHASE3, RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB
```

The new trial-noun/list relation checks cover both endpoints (`src/claims.py:559-575`, `:651-665`), but phase checking selects one nearest trial (`:779-785`) and status checking likewise selects one (`:848-851`). A trailing attribute selects the last trial; a prefix modifier selects the first listed trial. The assertion applies to both. Swapping the IDs in the recruiting-list example changes the result to False (`status: recruiting`). Elided `... evaluate osimertinib and are recruiting` also loses the group.

The original exact R1 group-status input is now rejected by `identity`, not by checking both statuses. Adding the faithful `trials of osimertinib` relation removes that incidental rejection and reveals the same ownership limitation. FIXED in the replay table is consequently an exact-input result, not a claim that the mechanism is safe.

Artifacts: `root4_confirmation.json` IDs `trial_group_status`, `trial_group_phase`, `trial_status_prefix`, `trial_status_swap`; [spec_findings.md](spec_findings.md), B4, including phase-prefix and elided controls. This requires multiple trial statements and is outside the listed residue.

### B5. Kind and clinical words borrow support from the wrong entity

| Q | Exact answer | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| Q3 | `EGFR is a drug inhibited by osimertinib.` | True; [] |
| Q3 control | `EGFR is a drug.` | False; `wording: drug` |
| Q4 | `PLB1003 inhibits ALK in clinical trials in this graph.` | True; [] |
| Q4 control | `PLB1003 has clinical trials.` | False; `wording: clinical`, `wording: trials` |

Q3 has only the osimertinib–EGFR inhibitor statement; it never calls EGFR a drug. `present` includes every entity kind anywhere in the clause (`src/claims.py:719-720`), and `:901-907` accepts `drug` whenever any Drug is present. The valid passive relation provides osimertinib and thus licenses a false kind claim about EGFR. The identity rule does not help because the clause contains a relation.

PLB1003's exact evidence is `PLB1003 acts on ALK (inhibitor; max stage PHASE_1; 0 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph)`. No Q4 statement contains `clinical`. The `wide` pool (`:745-759`) includes statements mentioning **any** clause entity, so ALK pulls in other drugs' positive trial counts. `about_trials` then accepts the new clinical-trials claim for PLB1003. A causal diagnostic checks the identical answer against only the unchanged PLB1003 row: it fails both `clinical` and `trials`; the full retrieval passes. The blocker itself uses the full normal retrieval.

These introduce `drug`/`clinical` absent from the relevant evidence and therefore are outside the one-statement vocabulary residue. Artifacts: `root4_confirmation.json` IDs `kind_drug`, `kind_drug_control`, `clinical_trial`, `clinical_trial_control`; [docs_diagnostics_results.json](docs_diagnostics_results.json), `trial_graph`, `trial_scope_full`, `trial_scope_plb_only`; [docs_findings.md](docs_findings.md), D-B2/D-B3.

### B6. Spelling out negation as letters is silently ignored

Q3 / `Osimertinib does N O T inhibit EGFR.` returns True; []. The lower-case `n o t` equivalent also passes. `src/claims.py:484-492` tokenizes the spelling as three single-letter words, and `:908` exempts all words shorter than two characters. This is a deliberately adversarial spelling, less natural than B1–B5; the verdict does not depend on it. It is nevertheless a reproduced unsupported negation, with additional text absent from the evidence and no matching explicit README residue. Ordinary `not`, `non-`, underscore and backtick controls remain rejected.

Artifacts: `root4_confirmation.json`, `split_negation`; `root4_probe.json`, `negation_split`, `non_ascii_hyphen`, `negation_underscore`, `negation_backtick`.

### S1. Faithful answers mostly pass, but new false positives remain

| Question | Original R1 corpus passed | R3 added corpus passed |
|---|---:|---:|

| Evidence answers | **29/30 (96.7%)** | **29/30 (96.7%)** |

These satisfy the tested **mostly-pass** part of the bar. New focused failures do not establish that most faithful answers fail:

| Q | Exact faithful answer | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| Q2 | `NCT07680790 and NCT02411448 are phase 3.` | False; `identity: NCT07680790 = NCT02411448` |
| Q5 | `AstraZeneca sponsors 14 trials of osimertinib (Tagrisso).` | False; `count_ambiguous: OSIMERTINIB / OSIMERTINIB` |
| Q4 | `LORLATINIB and CRIZOTINIB act on ALK. They are approved.` | False; `entity: They` |
| Q3 | `Osimertinib acts on EGFR [CHEMBL3353410, ENSG00000146648].` | False; `entity: ENSG00000146648` |
| Q2 | `Trials of osimertinib include NCT03909334 and NCT07680790.` | False; `fragment: OSIMERTINIB NCT03909334 NCT07680790` |

Causes and controls:

- `identity` (`src/claims.py:401-405`) treats coordinated subjects plus a shared copula as an equality. Both trials are PHASE3; spelling the statements separately passes.
- `count_ambiguous` (`:818-829`) counts mention positions, so a repeated alias is treated as another drug. Removing `(Tagrisso)` passes.
- The global capitalized-name test (`src/query.py:533-542`) has no `They` stop word. Lowercase `they` passes the same supported approval claim.
- Claim-level citation stripping accepts the comma-separated graph IDs, but the older global haystack (`query.py:482-487`) omits edge IDs and rejects ENSG. A single `[CHEMBL3353410]` citation passes. Space-separated valid IDs additionally mismatch `_strip_citations`' comma/semicolon split (`claims.py:211-218`).
- The trial-noun rule reads a trial before `of` (`claims.py:563-566`), whereas the faithful noun-list form puts the trials after `include`. The equivalent `Trials evaluating osimertinib include ...` passes.

The original `supports trials` wording and refusals naming France still fail as documented. The six specific R3 S1 repairs all pass. Exact inputs/outputs are in [replay4_report.md](replay4_report.md), [spec_findings.md](spec_findings.md), [docs_findings.md](docs_findings.md), and `root4_confirmation.json` / `root4_probe.json`.

### S2. Documentation improved, but the explicit known-residue list is incomplete

**Documentation/standards axis:** No separate repository coding-standard violation is asserted. The new `claims.py:10-11` explicitly calls its rules a description rather than a guarantee. `README.md:508-529`, `README.ko.md:354-365`, `src/query.py:448-466`, `web/template.html:263-267`, and `web/try.html:69-72,142-145` acknowledge pattern limitations and distinguish no findings from proof of truth. The original blanket documentation criticism should not be repeated unchanged.

However, the explicit known-pass list covers one-statement rearrangement, unknown non-INN drug names and odd evidence-consistent wording. It does not cover erased safety headings, cross-statement plural/count/status transfer, new kind/clinical words admitted through another entity, or ignored spelled-out negation. The broad unseen-shapes caveat is not the explicit known-residue disclosure required by this audit's approval bar. The heading claim is also materially incomplete: `claims.py:37-39` and README say headings are checked, but `_join` has already deleted the critical word in B1. These paths must be repaired or specifically disclosed.

Smaller precision issues: the docstring's nearest-entity phase summary (`claims.py:22-24`) does not reflect the new preference for the nearest **trial** (`:779-781`); `web/template.html:265` still says two adversarial rounds, while README describes three. The documentation-axis assessment is preserved separately in [docs_findings.md](docs_findings.md); the specification-axis assessment is in [spec_findings.md](spec_findings.md).

# Claim check — Codex round 4, finding by finding (2026-11-03 KST)

Report: `audit_evidence_claims/r4/report.md`, `r4_report.md`. Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED. All 438 earlier exact
reproductions were rejected; faithful corpora 29/30 and 29/30. Every new finding was reproduced here and was right.
Codex's probes were re-run from copies in `audit_evidence_claims/r4_recheck/`.

| # | Finding | Verdict | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B1 | "The following safe drugs:" — the heading join deleted "safe" | right | the slot is a determiner plus a plain noun only (optionally "NSCLC"); adjectives stay in the sentence and are checked |
| B2 | plural members lost after a per-member check, in object "them", in an elided subject | right | the group survives per-member checks; "them" after a verb stands for every entity of that kind in the clause before; an elided subject after a group is checked once per member; "they"/"it" after "targeted by A and B" stands for A and B |
| B3 | "trials: 10" skipped the shared-count check | right | a count after a trial word counts too; a repeated alias is one drug |
| B4 | a phase or status shared by two trials checked against one | right | a value belongs to the run of trials joined by "and" just before it (or the first run after it), and each must have it |
| B5 | "EGFR is a drug inhibited by …", "PLB1003 … in clinical trials" borrowed support | right | a kind word belongs to the entity just before it; trial/NSCLC context comes from the clause subject's own statements |
| B6 | "N O T" spelled out | right | two or more single letters in a row are reported as unreadable |
| S1 | faithful forms rejected (shared phase, alias in a count, "They", ENSG citation, "Trials of X include …") | right | identity only across a verb; aliases de-duplicated; pronouns in the stop list; the global name check drops citations whose ids are evidence records; trial lists after "include" |
| S2 | docs incomplete; dashboard said two rounds | right | docstring states the new ownership rules; README lists four rounds and the new shapes; dashboard text updated |

Found while re-checking: "Osimertinib inhibits EGFR and is EGFR" (identity through an elided subject), percentages read as plain
counts, and "1 phase" matching the digit of PHASE_1. All fixed and tested.

Tests 240 (R1 33 + R2 39 + R3 22 + R4 21 attacks; 46 faithful forms). Inputs from Codex's R4 probes that still pass are
consistent with the evidence ("ALK is targeted by them" after naming both ALK drugs, "Osimertinib inhibits it and targets it",
coordinated phases that are both PHASE3).

FILE src/claims.py
   1: """Clause-level claim check for a generated answer.
   2: 
   3: The name-and-number check in query.py catches invented drugs and invented
   4: figures. It cannot catch a false statement built from true tokens:
   5: "AstraZeneca has discontinued osimertinib", "osimertinib does not act on EGFR",
   6: "osimertinib is safe for all patients", "EGFR acts on osimertinib". This module
   7: reads each clause the way a reviewer would: who is it about, what does it say
   8: about them, and which retrieved statement says the same about the same entities?
   9: 
  10: What the check asks of each clause (a description of the rules, not a guarantee;
  11: see the last paragraph):
  12: 
  13:   * Relations (acts on / inhibits / activates, evaluates, sponsors) are read by
  14:     position: the entities before the relation word are its subject, the ones after
  15:     its object, reversed for a passive ("is inhibited by") and for "target of".
  16:     Both sides must be named (or be the subject carried over from the clause before),
  17:     must be the right kinds (a drug acts on a target, a trial evaluates a drug, a
  18:     company sponsors trials of a drug), and an evidence statement of that relation
  19:     must name both. Inhibit/activate must match the edge's action type.
  20:   * In a clause that states a relation, every entity it names must be part of a
  21:     relation that was checked.
  22:   * A phase belongs to the trials named just before it (a run joined by "and" shares
  23:     it), else to a drug's maximum stage; a figure belongs to the nearest entity after
  24:     it and must appear in evidence naming that entity and the clause's subject; a
  25:     trial count must appear as a trial count, a percentage as a percentage.
  26:   * A status belongs to the trial named before it. Words naming a kind of thing
  27:     ("drug", "company", "trial") need an entity of that kind or evidence about its
  28:     trials; "NSCLC" may only qualify trials the evidence calls NSCLC trials, never an
  29:     approval. A word naming a kind belongs to the entity just before it ("EGFR is a
  30:     drug" is reported). A trial count in a clause naming several drugs needs a count
  31:     per drug. Two different entities on either side of a verb that is no relation
  32:     ("X is Y") are reported. A plural pronoun, an elided subject after a group, and an
  33:     "it" after a group are checked once for every entity they stand for. Letters
  34:     spelled out one by one are reported. A heading's determiner ("these", "the
  35:     following") is replaced by the item, but adjectives in it stay and are checked.
  36:   * Every other content word must appear in evidence naming every entity of the
  37:     clause.
  38:   * Negations, comparisons, hedges and modal verbs, and claims of developing,
  39:     owning or marketing are reported; so are distinct products the resolver refuses,
  40:     pronouns with nothing to refer to, and text that is not English.
  41: 
  42: Exceptions, all narrow: a line that is a copy of a whole evidence statement (case
  43: and punctuation aside) passes as it stands, though any heading above it is still
  44: checked; a clause that names no entity and uses only words about the graph itself
  45: ("this graph cannot answer that question") passes; a bracket is dropped as a
  46: citation only when every id in it is a record of the retrieved evidence.
  47: 
  48: It is strict on purpose: a true fact the graph does not hold is reported as
  49: unverifiable, and a reviewer decides. It reads words and their order, not meaning,
  50: so a sentence it cannot parse into the shapes above can be misread; the known
  51: residue is listed in the README (known defect 13).
  52: """
