70:     def check(self, question: str, answer: str) -> dict:
71:         """Check an answer someone else wrote against the evidence for the question."""
72:         kg = self.kg
73:         entities = kg.link_entities(question)
74:         intent = kg.classify(question, entities)
75:         evidence = kg.retrieve(intent, entities)
76:         checks = kg.check_groundedness(answer, evidence, entities, generated=True,
77:                                        vocabulary=set(self.dic["index"]),
78:                                        linker=kg.link_entities)
79:         return {"intent": intent, "entities": entities, "checks": checks,
80:                 "evidence": [{"statement": e.statement, "source_record": e.source_record,
81:                               "source": e.source, "url": e.url} for e in evidence[:8]],

86:     class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
87:         def _send(self, code: int, body: bytes, ctype: str):
88:             self.send_response(code)
89:             self.send_header("Content-Type", ctype)
90:             self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
91:             self.end_headers()
92:             self.wfile.write(body)

94:         def do_GET(self):
95:             if self.path in ("/", "/index.html"):
96:                 self._send(200, PAGE.read_bytes(), "text/html; charset=utf-8")
97:             else:
98:                 self._send(404, b"not found", "text/plain")
99: 
100:         def do_POST(self):
101:             try:
102:                 n = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length") or 0)
103:                 body = json.loads(self.rfile.read(min(n, 20000)) or b"{}")
104:                 text = str(body.get("text") or "").strip()[:500]
105:                 if not text:
106:                     raise ValueError("empty input")
107:                 if self.path not in ("/api/resolve", "/api/ask", "/api/check"):
108:                     return self._send(404, b"{}", "application/json")
109:                 with engine.lock:
110:                     if self.path == "/api/resolve":
111:                         res = engine.resolve(text)
112:                     elif self.path == "/api/ask":
113:                         res = engine.ask(text)
114:                     else:
115:                         answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:4000]
116:                         if not answer:
117:                             raise ValueError("empty answer")
118:                         res = engine.check(text, answer)
119:                 self._send(200, json.dumps(res, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
120:                            "application/json; charset=utf-8")
121:             except Exception as e:  # show the reason on the page, not a dead request
122:                 self._send(400, json.dumps({"error": str(e)}, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
123:                            "application/json; charset=utf-8")

474: def _tokenise(unit, linker):
475:     """Entities and pronouns become placeholders."""
476:     s = unit
477:     for pat in SCOPE_PHRASES:
478:         s = re.sub(pat, " nsclc ", s, flags=re.I)
479:     s = s.replace("(s)", "s").replace("’", "'")
480:     s = re.sub(r"[()\[\]{}\"*—–]", " ", s)
481:     ents: list[dict] = []
482:     spans = []
483:     for m in RE_NCT.finditer(s):
484:         spans.append((m.start(), m.end(), {"type": "Trial", "label": m.group(0).upper()}))
485: 
486:     def blanked():
487:         b = list(s)
488:         for a, z, _ in spans:
489:             b[a:z] = " " * (z - a)
490:         # the linker strips only " ?.," from tokens, so "EGFR;" or "ALK:" would not link
491:         return re.sub(r"[;:!]", " ", "".join(b))
492: 
493:     # the linker reports each entity once, so "Osimertinib (Tagrisso)" needs a second look
494:     for _ in range(8):
495:         new = 0
496:         for e in linker(blanked()):
497:             if e.get("type") == "Disease":
498:                 continue
499:             words = [re.escape(w) for w in (e.get("matched_text") or "").split()]
500:             if not words:
501:                 continue
502:             pat = r"(?<![A-Za-z0-9])" + r"[\W_]+".join(words) + r"(?![A-Za-z0-9])"
503:             for m in re.finditer(pat, s, re.I):
504:                 if not any(a < m.end() and m.start() < b for a, b, _ in spans):
505:                     spans.append((m.start(), m.end(), e))
506:                     new += 1
507:         if not new:
508:             break
509:     low = s.lower()
510:     for phrase, typ in sorted(PRONOUN.items(), key=lambda x: -len(x[0])):
511:         for m in re.finditer(rf"\b{re.escape(phrase)}\b", low):
512:             if any(a < m.end() and m.start() < b for a, b, _ in spans):
513:                 continue
514:             after = low[m.end():]
515:             if re.match(r"\s+of\b", after) or any(
516:                     a >= m.end() and not s[m.end():a].strip(" ,") for a, _, _ in spans):

519:                                                "want": typ}))

535:             toks.append(("N", m.group(0).replace(",", "")))
536:         else:
537:             toks.append(("W", m.group(0)))
538:     return toks, ents
539: 
540: 
541: def _clauses(toks):
542:     """Split at ; and at and/but/while/whereas when both sides have a predicate."""
543:     joins = ("and", "but", "while", "whereas")
544:     cuts = [-1]
545:     for i, t in enumerate(toks):
546:         if t == ("P", ";"):
547:             cuts.append(i)
548:         elif t[0] == "W" and t[1] in joins:
549:             left = toks[cuts[-1] + 1:i]
550:             nxt = next((j for j in range(i + 1, len(toks))
551:                         if (toks[j][0] == "W" and toks[j][1] in joins)
552:                         or toks[j] == ("P", ";")), len(toks))
553:             right = toks[i + 1:nxt]
554:             if any(x[0] == "W" and x[1] in PREDICATE for x in left) and \
555:                     any(x[0] == "W" and x[1] in PREDICATE for x in right):
556:                 cuts.append(i)
557:     cuts.append(len(toks))
558:     return [toks[a + 1:b] for a, b in zip(cuts, cuts[1:]) if toks[a + 1:b]]
559: 
560: 
561: def _group(clause, k, step, skip=()):
562:     """Positions of the entity run nearest to k in direction `step`."""
563:     i = k + step
564:     while 0 <= i < len(clause) and (clause[i][0] != "ENT" or i in skip):
565:         i += step

FILE spec5_findings.md
# R5 independent semantic review

Reviewed HEAD `9fda6f8ec36e8b49d75e2d51d7f73ad3ba21e359`, diff `git diff 9fda6f8^...HEAD`, against the real graph through `Engine().check`. No tracked files changed. All new artifacts use the `spec5` prefix in `audit_evidence_claims/r5/`. Bytecode writes were disabled. The tests below judge support in the question's retrieved evidence; no external clinical truth is assumed.

## BLOCKERS

All passing attacks below return `grounded=True`, `applicable=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`.

### B1. A heading's erased noun still carries an unsupported kind or clinical claim

Question: `What does osimertinib target?`

Exact answers and observed output:

```text
The following companies inhibit EGFR:
- Osimertinib
```

True; [].

```text
These drugs:
- EGFR
```

True; [].

```text
The following NSCLC drugs inhibit EGFR:
- Osimertinib
```

True; [].

Other exact variants `These trials:\n- Osimertinib`, `These organisations inhibit EGFR:\n- Osimertinib`, and `These NSCLC trials:\n- Osimertinib` also pass. The sole evidence is `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`. It does not call osimertinib a company/trial, EGFR a drug, or osimertinib an NSCLC drug.

Cause: `src/claims.py:260-264` deletes the entire determiner-plus-noun slot, including optional `NSCLC`. The first answer becomes `Osimertinib inhibit EGFR`; there is no company word left for the kind validator. A plain noun can itself assert a false type. Adjective retention does not protect it.

Controls:

- `Companies inhibiting EGFR:\n- Osimertinib` -> False; `entity: Companies`, `wording: companies`.
- `The following drugs inhibit EGFR:\n- Osimertinib` -> True, correctly.
- `The following safe drugs inhibit EGFR:\n- Osimertinib` -> False; `wording: safe`.
- `These safe companies inhibit EGFR:\n- Osimertinib` -> False; `wording: companies`, `wording: safe`.
- `Osimertinib is an NSCLC drug.` -> False; `indication: lung cancer`.

The absent kind/disease words distinguish these cases from README's one-statement word-rearrangement residue. These are ordinary list headings, not artificial encodings.

Artifacts: `spec5_extended.json` IDs `heading_wrong_kind_companies`, `heading_wrong_kind_trials`, `heading_wrong_kind_drug`, `heading_wrong_kind_org`, `heading_wrong_kind_bare`, `heading_right_kind`, `heading_nsclc_rel`, `heading_nsclc_trial`, `heading_safe`, `heading_safe_company`; `spec5_controls.json` ID `nsclc_control`.

### B2. A relative clause redirects a kind claim to the wrong owner

Question: `What does osimertinib target?`

```text
EGFR, which is inhibited by osimertinib, is a drug.
```

True; []. The shorter `EGFR, inhibited by osimertinib, is a drug.` and `EGFR that is inhibited by osimertinib is a drug.` also pass. The main subject EGFR is explicitly called a drug. The single retrieved statement `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` has no word `drug` and identifies EGFR as the target through its edge/linker type.

Cause: `src/claims.py:987-988` assigns a kind word to the last entity mentioned anywhere before it. Here that is the drug osimertinib inside the relative clause, not EGFR, the subject of `is a drug`. `_clauses` only splits certain conjunctions/semicolons (`:541-558`); commas and relative clauses stay together. The supported passive relation covers the named entities, leaving the wrong kind attribution accepted.

Controls:

- `EGFR is a drug.` -> False; `wording: drug`.
- R4 exact `EGFR is a drug inhibited by osimertinib.` -> False; `wording: drug`.
- `EGFR is inhibited by osimertinib and is a drug.` -> False; `wording: drug`.
- Faithful inverse `Osimertinib, which inhibits EGFR, is a drug.` -> False; `wording: drug`.

This introduces a kind word absent from the evidence, then licenses it through the wrong entity; it is outside the explicitly listed one-statement vocabulary residue. The controls show both an unsupported pass and a faithful failure from the same positional rule.

Artifacts: `spec5_probe.json` IDs `kind_intervening`, `kind_intervening2`; `spec5_extended.json` IDs `kind_relative`, `kind_relative_that`, `kind_relative_drug_faithful`; `spec5_controls.json` IDs `kind_original_r4`, `kind_control_drug`.

### B3. A named subject group shares only the first member's trial context

Question: `Which drugs act on ALK?`

```text
LORLATINIB and PLB1003 have clinical trials in this graph.
```

True; []. Also:

```text
LORLATINIB and PLB1003 inhibit ALK in clinical trials.
```

True; []. The retrieved rows explicitly state:

```text
LORLATINIB acts on ALK (inhibitor; max stage APPROVAL; 13 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph)
PLB1003 acts on ALK (inhibitor; max stage PHASE_1; 0 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph)
```

The first row supports lorlatinib's trial context, while PLB1003 has zero in this graph. No retrieved row contains `clinical`. These are unsupported group assertions rather than harmless statements that both drugs inhibit ALK.

Cause: `_check_attributes` chooses `subject = subjects[0]` (`src/claims.py:771`), and its new `wide` pool selects only rows mentioning that first subject (`:802`). `about_trials` is then shared by all kind/clinical words (`:808-811`, `:1013-1018`). Explicit named groups are not expanded once per member in `_check_unit`; the per-member paths at `:383-398` handle pronouns and elided subjects only.

Controls:

- `PLB1003 and LORLATINIB have clinical trials in this graph.` -> False; `wording: clinical`, `wording: trials`.
- `PLB1003 and LORLATINIB inhibit ALK in clinical trials.` -> False; same findings.
- `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 inhibit ALK. They are in clinical trials.` -> False; same findings.
- `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 inhibit ALK and have clinical trials.` -> False; same findings.
- `LORLATINIB and CRIZOTINIB inhibit ALK in clinical trials.` -> True; evidence-consistent control.
- R4 exact `PLB1003 inhibits ALK in clinical trials in this graph.` -> False; both wording findings.

The group can also borrow NSCLC context: `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 have NSCLC trials.` -> True; reversed order -> False (`indication: lung cancer`, `wording: trials`). The clinical-plus-graph case is the strongest reproduction because the new word is absent from all evidence and the graph counts explicitly disprove the group assertion. It requires two drug rows, outside the one-statement residue.

Artifacts: `spec5_probe.json` IDs `group_clinical`, `group_clinical_reverse`, `group_clinical_pron`, `group_both_clinical`; `spec5_extended.json` IDs `group_shared_clinical`, `group_shared_trials`, `group_shared_nsclc`, `group_shared_nsclc_reverse`, `group_shared_clinical_pron_survive`, `group_shared_clinical_elided`; `spec5_controls.json` IDs `clinical_group_graph`, `clinical_group_graph_reverse`, `clinical_original_r4`.

### B4. The documented `it` repair does not cover a passive list

Question: `Which drugs act on ALK?`

```text
ALK is targeted by LORLATINIB and PLB1003. It is approved.
```

True; []. Changing `It` to `They` returns False; `wording: approved`.

`ALK is targeted by LORLATINIB and PLB1003. It has clinical trials.` also passes. With `They have clinical trials`, the checker rejects clinical/trials for PLB1003. `ALK is targeted by LORLATINIB and PLB1003 and is approved.` passes too.

Cause: `st.group` is the grammatical subject group `[ALK]`. At `src/claims.py:368-369`, selection of the biggest type group in `st.last` only occurs when `p['label'] in PLURAL`; `it` is not in `PLURAL` (`:155`). It therefore resolves to ALK (`:377-379`). ALK then pulls in all ALK rows, including other drugs' APPROVAL and positive trial counts. This differs from `CODEX_CLAIMS_R4_VERDICT.md` B2, which explicitly says `they`/`it` after `targeted by A and B` stands for A and B, and from the docstring's per-member `it` claim at `src/claims.py:32-33`.

If `It` is read as the grammatical subject ALK, approval is unsupported; if read as one of the drugs, PLB1003 does not have APPROVAL. The conservative rule documented by this repair promises checking all candidate group members, but does not do so here. This is at least a confirmed code/documentation repair discrepancy. The verdict does not depend on treating this case as outside the broad rearrangement residue: B1-B3 are stronger, unambiguous outside-residue failures.

Artifacts: `spec5_probe.json` IDs `passive_it_approval`, `passive_they_approval`; `spec5_extended.json` IDs `passive_it_clinical`, `passive_it_clinical_reverse`, `passive_they_clinical`, `group_shared_clinical_subjectless_after_passive`, `object_them_then_it`.

## SHOULD FIX

Faithful ordinary kind phrases are rejected by the same nearest-entity rule:

Question `What does osimertinib target?`:

- `EGFR is inhibited by the drug osimertinib.` -> False; `wording: drug`. The kind word occurs before its named noun but after EGFR, so it binds to EGFR.
- `Osimertinib, which inhibits EGFR, is a drug.` -> False; `wording: drug`. The kind word binds to EGFR in the relative clause.
- Control `The drug osimertinib inhibits EGFR.` -> True.

These are focused false-positive controls, not a sampled corpus and not a claim about most answers. The parent's full faithful corpus review determines that part of the approval bar. Source: `src/claims.py:981-991`; artifacts `spec5_probe.json` ID `kind_faithful`, `spec5_extended.json` IDs `kind_relative_drug_faithful`, `kind_before_noun_faithful`.

# R5 documentation axis, citation attack and fresh faithful corpus

Reviewed HEAD `9fda6f8`, `git diff 9fda6f8^...HEAD`, against the local graph. All new files in this sub-review are `audit_evidence_claims/r5/docs5*`. No tracked file was edited. `Engine().check(question, answer)` was used for every case, with full actual retrieved evidence saved beside each result. No external medical claim or network lookup is required: support is judged solely against these records.

## BLOCKERS

### D-B1. A kind noun before its entity borrows the kind of a later entity

Exact observed cases (all `grounded=True`, `applicable=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`):

| Question | Exact answer |
|---|---|
| `What does osimertinib target?` | `The drug EGFR is inhibited by osimertinib.` |
| `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?` | `The drug AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib.` |
| `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?` | `The company osimertinib is sponsored by AstraZeneca.` |

The relation is supported in each case, but the entity-kind assertion is not. In Q3, the entire evidence is `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`; the linker identifies EGFR as Target and osimertinib as Drug. The added word `drug` is absent from that statement, so this does not qualify for README item 13's one-statement rearrangement residue. The Q5 rows record AstraZeneca as the sponsor and osimertinib as the evaluated drug; the added wrong kind does not occur in those statements either.

Cause: `src/claims.py:983-988` gives a kind word to the previous entity if present, otherwise accepts that kind anywhere in the clause (`kind in present`). A noun before its own name is ordinary English, but the wrong later Drug/Company licenses it. This is a fresh variant of R4 B5, whose exact post-entity input was repaired.

Controls, all executed: `EGFR is a drug inhibited by osimertinib.` -> False, `wording: drug`; `AstraZeneca is a drug and sponsors osimertinib.` -> False, `wording: drug`; `EGFR is inhibited by osimertinib.` -> True; `The drug osimertinib inhibits EGFR.` -> True. `EGFR is inhibited by the drug osimertinib.` is a faithful false positive -> False, `wording: drug`, because nearest preceding EGFR is selected even though the noun modifies the next entity.

Artifact: `docs5_controls_results.json`, IDs `kind_prefix_target` through `kind_prefix_drug_as_company`, and `faithful_passive_kind` / `faithful_passive_nokind`.

### D-B2. Evidence that a drug is evaluated in trials licenses calling the drug a trial

Question: `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?`

- `Osimertinib is a clinical trial.` -> True; [].
- `Tagrisso is a clinical trial.` -> True; [].
- `Osimertinib is a trial.` -> True; [].

The evidence records named NCT trials evaluating OSIMERTINIB. It does not identify the drug as a trial. The only statement words are trial IDs, registry phase/status and `evaluates OSIMERTINIB`; `clinical` and `trial` in the new assertion are not borrowed statement words. This is outside the explicit one-statement residue. No medical judgment beyond the graph's Drug/Trial types is involved.

Cause: `src/claims.py:808-811` turns any subject-owned EVALUATES row into `about_trials=True`; `:1013-1018` handles `trial`, `trials` and `clinical` only via that Boolean, exempting them from the owner/kind test at `:983-988`. It cannot distinguish `is a clinical trial` from `is in clinical trials`. The identity check at `:443-449` needs entities on both sides of a verb and does not see a kind noun as a second entity.

Controls: the identical `Osimertinib is a clinical trial.` against `What does osimertinib target?` -> False, `wording: clinical`, `wording: trial`; the faithful Q2 inputs `Osimertinib is in clinical trials.` and `NCT07680790 is a clinical trial.` both pass. These controls isolate the existence-of-trials shortcut.

Artifact: `docs5_controls_results.json`, IDs `trial_identity` through `trial_identity_drugs_both`.

## SHOULD FIX

### D-S1. Fresh faithful prose still has material coverage gaps

The fixed 40-answer corpus was authored before executing it: eight answers per demo evidence question, plus six separate refusals. All 40 were checked against the actual evidence. Counts are separate per-drug counts, not new sums; no clinical indication, safety or inferred combination-trial count is introduced. `docs5_faithful_evidence_review.json` records each exact answer and the evidence basis. Some synonyms/paraphrases require ordinary reading (sponsorship/runs, inhibitor/inhibition), which is precisely the intended faithful-prose test.

| Question | Passed |
|---|---:|
| Q1: companies developing osimertinib (answers correctly describe sponsorship) | 4/8 |
| Q2: trials evaluating Tagrisso | 7/8 |
| Q3: osimertinib target | 5/8 |
| Q4: ALK drugs | 7/8 |
| Q5: AstraZeneca in NSCLC | 5/8 |
| Evidence answers | **28/40 (70%)** |
| Q6 refusals, separate | **5/6** |

This diagnostic sample still passes a majority; it does not show that most production LLM answers fail and is not a statistically representative sample. Do not pool it with the historical curated corpora. The sponsorship-not-development caution is already explicitly documented as over-strict at README.md:525-527. Other failures are ordinary relation, pronoun, count or status phrasings.

Every failed evidence answer, with exact observed finding pairs:

| ID | Exact answer | Observed findings (grounded=False) |
|---|---|---|
| faithful_q1_5 | `The graph records sponsorship of osimertinib trials by AstraZeneca.` | fragment: OSIMERTINIB Astrazeneca |
| faithful_q1_6 | `Sponsors of osimertinib trials include AstraZeneca and Bristol Myers Squibb.` | unrelated_entity: Astrazeneca; unrelated_entity: Bristol Myers Squibb; relation_direction: OSIMERTINIB sponsors OSIMERTINIB |
| faithful_q1_7 | `AstraZeneca sponsors 14 trials of osimertinib. Bristol Myers Squibb sponsors 2 trials of it.` | number: 2; relation_direction: Bristol Myers Squibb sponsors Astrazeneca |
| faithful_q1_8 | `AstraZeneca sponsors trials evaluating osimertinib. This is evidence of trial sponsorship, not drug development.` | wording: development; wording: drug; negation: not |
| faithful_q2_7 | `NCT07680790 is recruiting. NCT03909334 is active but not recruiting.` | wording: active; wording: but; negation: not; status: recruiting |
| faithful_q3_4 | `The graph records osimertinib acting on EGFR as an inhibitor.` | relation_incomplete: inhibitor |
| faithful_q3_6 | `Reported mechanism: inhibition of EGFR by osimertinib.` | unrelated_entity: OSIMERTINIB |
| faithful_q3_8 | `Osimertinib acts on EGFR; its recorded action is inhibition.` | wording: action; relation_incomplete: inhibition |
| faithful_q4_5 | `The graph records 13 NSCLC trials of lorlatinib and 15 NSCLC trials of crizotinib.` | number: 15 |
| faithful_q5_6 | `AstraZeneca sponsors 7 NSCLC trials evaluating durvalumab and 8 evaluating gefitinib.` | relation_direction: Astrazeneca evaluating; unrelated_entity: GEFITINIB |
| faithful_q5_7 | `Reported sponsorship:\n- AstraZeneca: 14 NSCLC trials of osimertinib.\n- AstraZeneca: 10 NSCLC trials of carboplatin.` | fragment: Astrazeneca CARBOPLATIN; fragment: Astrazeneca OSIMERTINIB |
| faithful_q5_8 | `AstraZeneca runs 6 NSCLC trials of pemetrexed and 6 of cisplatin.` | count_ambiguous: PEMETREXED / CISPLATIN |

Causal controls from `docs5_controls_results.json`: naming `osimertinib` instead of `it` after `2 trials of` turns `faithful_q1_7` into True; breaking `faithful_q4_5` into two graph-count sentences turns it into True; replacing `active but not recruiting` with `active, not recruiting` turns that status claim into True. The pronoun rule at `claims.py:355-379` only infers object kind from a relation verb in the last two words, so `trials of it` inherits AstraZeneca. The count scope at `:771,784-787` forces the first named drug into the second drug's count. The status recognizer at `:928-945` requires the literal status word sequence; inserting `but` leaves `active` / `not` unsupported and separately treats `recruiting` as a positive claim.

The separate refusal `I cannot determine the capital of France from this graph.` is False (`entity: France`; `no_subject: i cannot determine capital france`) as explicitly documented in README item 13.

### D-S2. Documentation axis: improved qualifications, remaining concrete mismatch

No separate repository coding-standard violation is asserted. The user supplied the review standard, and the reviewed changes are limited to claims/query/tests/docs. The existing caveats should not be reported as absent:

- `README.md:508-531` / `README.ko.md:354-366` explicitly identify pattern reading and the accepted residues. They now mention four adversarial rounds and the relevant R4 shapes.
- `src/claims.py:10-11,48-51` labels the docstring a description, not a guarantee. `src/query.py:450-468` similarly distinguishes generated checking from template applicability and links known residue.
- `web/template.html:263-267` now says four rounds. `web/try.html:69-72,142-145` explicitly warns that no findings are not proof of truth; no blanket-success wording remains.
- Citation validation is now applied before global-name checking (`src/query.py:482-489`) and the tested claim matches the observed results below.

However, the explicit known-pass list in README.md:520-524 / README.ko.md:361-363 does not disclose D-B1's new kind word accepted through another entity or D-B2's false Drug-is-Trial assertion using clinical/kind words absent from the statements. The broad unseen-shape caveat does not meet this audit's requirement to disclose each *known* residue. `claims.py:26-30` describes kind checking, but the kind rules only distinguish a prefix from a previous entity and exempt trial-kind identity.

Small precision issues, separate from the verdict-driving clinical/kind passes:

- `claims.py:22-25` only summarizes phase ownership before a phase, whereas the actual `trial_run` also uses the first trial run after a prefix phase (`:814-825`). Likewise the status summary at `:26` says a singular prior trial, whereas a run is checked, including a following run.
- `CODEX_CLAIMS_R4_VERDICT.md:3` says all 438 earlier exact reproductions were rejected. The prior report's 438 counts include faithful answers and controls, so this must mean prior *attack reproductions*, not all 438 cases. The historical replay provides the exact denominators.
- `claims.py:33-34` says spelled-out letters are reported, but comma-separated examples below pass. This is a narrow documentation/rule mismatch, not needed to establish the stronger natural-language blockers.

The known-ID set comes from exact source, edge and statement tokens (`claims.py:304-308`, `query.py:486-489`), and `_strip_citations` requires every parsed ID (`claims.py:216-223`). Newline and space delimiters correctly retain the same allowed IDs. Exact full outputs are in `docs5_probe_results.json`, `cite_*` IDs. The passing `[Osimertinib](is-safe-and-discontinued) acts on EGFR.` has a faithful visible claim and an artificial non-URL link destination; it is not an unsupported visible clinical claim and is not a blocker.

### N2. Comma-separated spelling is a secondary rule/documentation mismatch

Question Q3: `Osimertinib does N, O, T inhibit EGFR.` -> True; []; `Osimertinib is S, A, F, E.` -> True; []. Their whitespace-only equivalents are False with `unreadable: spelled-out letters`. The commas are `P` tokens and reset the single-letter run (`claims.py:970-979`); all leftover one-letter words are skipped (`:1020`). Slash/dot/hyphen spelling was rejected in the main probe. These are artificial encodings, so they are reported as secondary notes; the docstring's explicit spelled-letter rule is overbroad. They do not drive the verdict because D-B1/D-B2 are ordinary grammatical claims.

### N3. Evidence-consistent or explicitly disclosed passes do not block

Q2 `Osimertinib is recruiting.` passes but is a one-statement word/entity rearrangement and is not used as an independent blocker under the stated residue bar. Plain supported target relations and citations pass. Ordinary Q3 `Osimertinib is safe.` and `Osimertinib is discontinued.` remain False (`wording`).

+### B1. Heading substitution still deletes kind and disease claims

+### B2. Named groups borrow the first member's clinical-trial context
+
+Q4:
+
+```text
+LORLATINIB and PLB1003 are in clinical trials.
+```
+
+True; []. The more explicit `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 have clinical trials in this graph.` also passes, as does `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 inhibit ALK in clinical trials.`
+
+Retrieved evidence:
+
+```text
+LORLATINIB acts on ALK (inhibitor; max stage APPROVAL; 13 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph)
+PLB1003 acts on ALK (inhibitor; max stage PHASE_1; 0 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph)
+```
+
+No Q4 statement contains `clinical`. PLB1003's row explicitly gives zero trials in this graph. The shared clinical-trial assertion therefore borrows another drug's positive context; it is not merely the supported assertion that both inhibit ALK.
+
+**Cause:** `_check_attributes` picks `subjects[0]` at `src/claims.py:771`; `wide` is now scoped only to that first subject at `:802`. `about_trials` then licenses all clinical/trial words at `:808-811,1013-1018`. Explicit named subject groups are not expanded by the per-member paths at `:383-398`, which handle pronouns and elision.

They are in clinical trials.` also rejects. Supported LORLATINIB + CRIZOTINIB clinical context passes. This order dependence isolates the first-member leak, outside the one-statement residue.

+### B3. `It` after a passive list still misses the documented group check
+
+Q4:
+
+```text
+ALK is targeted by LORLATINIB and PLB1003. It is approved.
+```
+
+True; []. Changing `It` to `They` rejects `wording: approved`. Using the active first sentence, `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 target ALK. It is approved.`, also rejects. The faithful passive LORLATINIB + CRIZOTINIB list followed by `They are approved.` passes.
+
+**Cause:** the passive sentence leaves `st.group` as `[ALK]`. The largest same-type group from `st.last` is selected at `src/claims.py:368-369` only when the pronoun belongs to `PLURAL`; `it` does not (`:155`). It resolves to ALK at `:377-379`, whose evidence pool contains other drugs' approval. This is the opposite of the explicit B2 repair claim in `CODEX_CLAIMS_R4_VERDICT.md:11` and the per-member `it` description in `claims.py:32-33`.
+
+Approval of the target ALK is unsupported, and the documented conservative interpretation of the two-drug list must also reject because PLB1003 has only PHASE_1. Because the pronoun can be semantically ambiguous and the later clause can resemble the broad single-row residue, this is principally a **confirmed repair/code/documentation discrepancy**, not the sole basis for the verdict. B1, B2, B4, B5 and B6 independently fail the outside-residue bar.
+
+Artifacts: `root5_confirmation.json`, `B3_*`; `spec5_probe.json`, `passive_it_approval` / `passive_they_approval`.
+
+### B4. Parenthetical metadata breaks shared trial phase/status ownership
+
+Q2, both pass:
+
+```text
+NCT03909334 (phase 2) and NCT07680790 (phase 3) are recruiting.
+```
+
+```text
+NCT03909334 (active, not recruiting) and NCT07680790 are phase 3.
+```
+
+Evidence:
+
+```text
+NCT03909334 (PHASE2, ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB
+NCT07680790 (PHASE3, RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB
+```
+
+The first answer incorrectly calls NCT03909334 recruiting; the second assigns it phase 3. Each uses a natural parenthetical descriptor that is itself correct.
+
+**Cause:** `trial_run` at `src/claims.py:814-826` asks `_group` for a contiguous run around the nearest trial. The intervening phase/status tokens stop the run before the earlier trial (`_group`, `:561-580`). Shared phase validation at `:849-860` or shared status validation at `:935-947` therefore checks only the last trial. The named subjects still include both trials; no general rule requires their shared attributes to cover the full subject list.
+
+Controls: removing the parentheticals makes both claims fail (`status: recruiting` / `phase: phase 3`). Reversing the first answer's trial order makes it fail `status: recruiting`. The faithful `NCT07680790 and NCT02411448 are phase 3.` passes. This transfers values across two records, outside the explicit one-statement residue.
+
+Artifacts: `root5_confirmation.json`, `B4_*`; `root5_numeric.json`, `status_phase_parentheses`, `status_phase_one`, `phase_context_parentheses`, and controls.
+
+### B5. Counts escape through longer labels and cross-sentence references
+
+Q5:
+
+```text
+AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib and carboplatin in trials in this graph: 10.
+```
+
+True; []. The evidence has **14** trials for OSIMERTINIB and **10** for CARBOPLATIN; it does not establish a shared ten-trial count. The exact R4 immediate label `... in trials: 10.` now rejects `count_ambiguous`, but ordinary inserted words bypass it.
+
+**Cause:** trial-count detection only examines three surrounding tokens (`src/claims.py:878-881`), while shared-count detection examines three after/two before (`:905-907`). `in this graph:` pushes `trials` out of both windows. The number is treated as an untyped figure and validated against the nearest drug, CARBOPLATIN, using `scoped(..., "after")` (`:777-789,882-889`). `... in trials recorded: 10.` also passes. Correct separate `14 trials of osimertinib and 10 trials of carboplatin` passes; swapped counts reject both numbers.
+
+A more natural cross-sentence variant also passes:
+
+```text
+AstraZeneca sponsors trials of Tagrisso. There are 10 such trials in this graph.
+```
+
+The faithful 14 version passes too. Replacing the second sentence with `This drug has 10 such trials in this graph.` rejects number 10. `AstraZeneca sponsors trials of Tagrisso and has 10 such trials.` also passes. `such` refers back to osimertinib trials, but the state retains AstraZeneca as the subject (`:470-471,281-288`), the elided-subject rule reuses it (`:427-433`), and numeric scope then needs only the sponsor, allowing CARBOPLATIN's row. There is no retained sponsor-drug pair for `such trials`. This is value transfer across two statements, not a harmless rewording of one record.
+
+Artifacts: `root5_confirmation.json`, `B5_*`; `root5_numeric.json`, `count_label_*`, `count_such_*`, `count_separate_*`.
+
+### B6. Kind words still accept the wrong owner or confuse participation with identity
+
+| Q | Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q3 | `EGFR, which is inhibited by osimertinib, is a drug.` | True; [] |
+| Q3 | `The drug EGFR is inhibited by osimertinib.` | True; [] |
+| Q2 | `Osimertinib is a clinical trial.` | True; [] |
+
+The first two call the target EGFR a drug; the third calls a drug a trial. The Q3 evidence is only `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`. Q2 evidence consists of named NCT trials evaluating OSIMERTINIB. None supports these identities. `drug` in the Q3 assertion and `clinical trial` in Q2 are absent from the respective evidence statements, so these are outside the explicit one-statement vocabulary residue.
+
+Three concrete paths:
+
+- At `src/claims.py:987-988`, a kind word binds to the last entity mentioned anywhere before it. A relative clause can put OSIMERTINIB nearer than the main subject EGFR.
+- With no preceding entity, the same line accepts `kind in present`, letting the Drug at the other end of the supported relation license `The drug EGFR`.
+- `trial`, `trials`, and `clinical` skip that owner test (`:983-985`) and use `about_trials` (`:808-811,1013-1018`). The checker treats `is a trial` like `is in trials`; identity validation requires a named entity on each side (`:443-449`), so it does not intervene.
+
+Controls: R4 `EGFR is a drug inhibited by osimertinib.` rejects `wording: drug`; Q3 `Osimertinib is a clinical trial.` rejects `clinical` and `trial`; faithful Q2 `Osimertinib is in clinical trials.` and `NCT07680790 is a clinical trial.` pass. The same owner rule also rejects faithful `EGFR is inhibited by the drug osimertinib.` and `Osimertinib, which inhibits EGFR, is a drug.` with `wording: drug`.
+
+Artifacts: `root5_confirmation.json`, `B6_*`, `S1_*`; [docs5_controls_results.json](docs5_controls_results.json), `kind_prefix_*`, `trial_identity*`; [docs5_findings.md](docs5_findings.md).
+

+### S2. Documentation axis: better caveats, but incomplete explicit residues and repair claims
+
+No separate repository coding-standard violation is asserted. The specification-axis findings are B1-B6; this documentation assessment is separate.
+
+The earlier blanket criticism should not be repeated: `README.md:508-531`, `README.ko.md:354-366`, `claims.py:10-11,48-51`, `query.py:450-468`, `web/template.html:263-267`, and `web/try.html:69-72,142-145` acknowledge pattern limitations and distinguish no findings from proof of truth. The page now correctly says four rounds. Evidence-validated citation stripping in the global name check behaved as documented in the new controls.
+
+However, the explicit known-pass list (`README.md:520-524`, `README.ko.md:361-363`) does not disclose the heading kind/disease erasure, named-group clinical borrowing, cross-record trial/status/count transfer, or new false kind assertions above. A general unseen-shapes caveat is not disclosure of these now-known residues under this audit's bar.
+
+Concrete precision issues:
+
+- `CODEX_CLAIMS_R4_VERDICT.md:11` says `they`/`it` after a passive list stand for both drugs; B3 shows the `it` path does not.
+- `claims.py:33-35` broadly describes spelled-out letter rejection and heading preservation. Comma-separated spelling still passes, and the heading's noun/NSCLC modifier can be removed (B1).
+- `claims.py:25` and `CODEX_CLAIMS_R4_VERDICT.md:20-21` describe percentage checking/fixing; a space before `%` still defeats it (N2). The guarantee needs this qualification even though this audit conservatively treats the one-row numeric variant as a residue note.
+- `claims.py:22-26` describes prior-trial ownership less precisely than `trial_run`, which also handles a following run.
+- `CODEX_CLAIMS_R4_VERDICT.md:3` says all 438 earlier exact reproductions were rejected, but that denominator included faithful/control cases. It should identify the rejected **attack** cases rather than all 438.
+
+The original exact repairs are genuine; the documentation's problem is the scope claimed for them and omission of newly demonstrated residues.

+
+### N2. Secondary numeric/spelling discrepancies; not independent verdict drivers
+
+Q4 `LORLATINIB has 13 % NSCLC trials.` and its nonbreaking-space version pass; attached `13%` rejects `number: 13%`. `RE_TOKEN` only captures a percent sign attached to digits (`src/claims.py:180-181`); a separate sign is discarded, leaving an ordinary supported count. `root5_numeric.json` records both. Because R4 already treated the one-row percentage transformation as plausibly covered by the broad residue, this review does not promote it to an independent blocker merely because R5 tried to fix it. The blanket percentage-repair statement remains inaccurate.
+
+Q3 `Osimertinib does N, O, T inhibit EGFR.` and `Osimertinib is S, A, F, E.` pass, while whitespace-only letters reject. Commas reset the run at `claims.py:970-979`, and leftover single letters are skipped at `:1020`. These artificial forms are secondary notes about the explicitly stated letter rule, not a basis needed for rejection. Exact outputs are in `docs5_probe_results.json` / `docs5_controls_results.json`.
+
+Evidence-consistent passes are not blockers: supported active/passive inhibition, correct group approval, alias identity, valid citations and correct paired per-drug counts. Q2 `Osimertinib is recruiting.` is conservatively assigned to the disclosed one-statement rearrangement residue, not counted as a new independent blocker.
+

+
+- `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **240 passed in 8.01s**, exit 0, empty stderr. TEMP/TMP were confined to `r5/replay5_temp`; bytecode and pytest cache writes were disabled.
+- **5/5 evidence template prose bodies pass**, with template headings and the same `Scope:` removal used by the normal path. Scope-inclusive templates and the generated-check France refusal remain rejected; the ordinary template/refusal path is separate. Exact text is in `replay5_faithful_results.json`.
+- Dictionary: **7,610 keys / 1,066 drugs / 2,634 unique multiword aliases**. Original short-word-gate replay loses **zero** intended links; `is a` stays unlinked. `AZD 9291`, `osimertinib mesylate`, `trastuzumab deruxtecan` and `pemetrexed disodium` retain their links. Five previously documented dictionary-to-tokenizer discrepancies are unchanged, not new R5 regressions.
+- `Osimertinib is safe. EGFR is safe.` preserves two separate contexts. Same-unit `Osimertinib is safe and EGFR is safe.` still merges duplicate `safe` values, but remains False; dedup does not turn a nonempty finding list into a pass.
+- ALK question punctuation with `!`, `:` or `;` remains an unchanged linker limitation; `?` works.
